            "fallback": "Each event receives failure budget one over 120, and its fixed-index tail probability is bounded by that share."

            "title": "Join the events once",

        "title": "Explore-Then-Commit",
        "kind": "algorithm",
        "steps": [
          {
            "title": "Explore",
            "detail": "Pull every arm m times, usually in round-robin order."
          },
          {
            "title": "Estimate",
            "detail": "Compute each arm's empirical mean from its exploration samples."
          },
          {
            "title": "Commit",
            "detail": "Choose an empirical maximizer using a documented tie rule."
          },
          {
            "title": "Exploit",
            "detail": "Pull that chosen arm for every remaining round."
          }

        "pseudocode": {
          "title": "Textbook Explore-Then-Commit",
          "intro": "This version makes the exploration budget, commit time, empirical means, and tie convention explicit before the regret theorem is read.",
          "formula_label": "Empirical winner after exploration",
          "math": "\\widehat\\mu_a=\\frac1m\\sum_{s=1}^{m}X_{a,s},\\qquad \\widehat a=\\min\\operatorname*{arg\\,max}_{a\\in[k]}\\widehat\\mu_a.",
          "fallback": "Average the first m samples of each arm and commit to the least-encoded arm with the largest empirical mean.",
          "lines": [
            "input: k arms, horizon n, exploration count m with 1 ≤ m ≤ n/k",

            "set a_hat to the least-encoded empirical-mean maximizer",
            "for every remaining round: pull a_hat and record its reward"
          ],
          "relationship": "The textbook permits any fixed empirical-mean tie rule.       "algorithm": {
        "title": "OFUL confidence-ellipsoid loop",
        "kind": "algorithm",
        "steps": [
          {
            "title": "Update geometry",
            "detail": "Accumulate the regularized feature Gram matrix from past actions."
          },
          {
            "title": "Estimate",
            "detail": "Compute the ridge least-squares parameter estimate."
          },
          {
            "title": "Build confidence",
            "detail": "Place plausible parameters in an ellipsoid around the estimate."
          },
          {
            "title": "Act optimistically",
            "detail": "Choose the action and plausible parameter pair with the largest predicted reward."
          },
          {
            "title": "Charge widths",
            "detail": "Bound regret by confidence widths and use the elliptical-potential sum."
          }
        ],
        "pseudocode": {
          "title": "OFUL optimistic ridge loop",
          "intro": "The compact loop separates the state carried between rounds from the confidence maximization performed at the current round.",
          "formula_label": "Ridge estimate and optimistic score",
          "math": "V_t=\\lambda I+\\sum_{s<t}x_sx_s^\\top,\\quad \\widehat\\theta_t=V_t^{-1}\\sum_{s<t}x_sy_s,\\quad x_t\\in\\operatorname*{arg\\,max}_{x\\in\\mathcal D_t}\\left(\\langle\\widehat\\theta_t,x\\rangle+\\beta_t\\lVert x\\rVert_{V_t^{-1}}\\right).",
          "fallback": "Form the regularized Gram matrix and ridge estimate, then choose the feasible action with the largest estimated reward plus its confidence width.",
          "lines": [
            "input: regularization λ > 0, confidence δ, feasible action sets D_t",
            "initialize V_1 = λ I and b_1 = 0",
            "for t = 1,…,n:",
            "    set θ̂_t = V_t^{-1} b_t and compute confidence radius β_t",
            "    choose a documented maximizer of <θ̂_t,x> + β_t ||x||_{V_t^{-1}}",
            "    observe y_t; update V_{t+1} = V_t + x_t x_t^T and b_{t+1} = b_t + x_t y_t"
          ],
          "relationship": "The source algorithm permits time-varying feasible sets.         "title": "How confidence geometry pays for linear-bandit regret",
        "summary": "OFUL replaces one confidence interval per arm by one ellipsoid for the unknown parameter. The same inverse-Gram norm measures both uncertainty and the cost charged to the trajectory.",
        "steps": [
          {
            "title": "Put the parameter in an ellipsoid",

        "title": "Known-reward Hoeffding UCBVI-CH",
        "kind": "algorithm",
        "steps": [
          {
            "title": "Count strict prefixes",
            "detail": "Before episode k, aggregate visits and next-state transitions from episodes strictly earlier than k."
          },

            "title": "Estimate transitions",

            "detail": "Add a Hoeffding bonus, clip against the horizon and previous Q table, and run backward dynamic programming."
          },

            "title": "Execute the greedy policy",
            "detail": "Generate the next episode with the measurable greedy policy from that recurrent table."

            "title": "Assemble regret",

Invertible transport assumes a fixed c>0; the two real calculus helpers allow arbitrary c.
